```ini
# A generic, single database configuration.

[alembic]
# path to migration scripts.
# this is typically a path given in POSIX (e.g. forward slashes)
# format, relative to the token %(here)s which refers to the location of this
# ini file
script_location = %(here)s/alembic

# template used to generate migration file names; The default value is %%(rev)s_%%(slug)s
# Uncomment the line below if you want the files to be prepended with date and time
# see https://alembic.sqlalchemy.org/en/latest/tutorial.html#editing-the-ini-file
# for all available tokens
# file_template = %%(year)d_%%(month).2d_%%(day).2d_%%(hour).2d%%(minute).2d-%%(rev)s_%%(slug)s
# Or organize into date-based subdirectories (requires recursive_version_locations = true)
# file_template = %%(year)d/%%(month).2d/%%(day).2d_%%(hour).2d%%(minute).2d_%%(second).2d_%%(rev)s_%%(slug)s

# sys.path path, will be prepended to sys.path if present.
# defaults to the current working directory.  for multiple paths, the path separator
# is defined by "path_separator" below.
prepend_sys_path = .


# timezone to use when rendering the date within the migration file
# as well as the filename.
# If specified, requires the tzdata library which can be installed by adding
# `alembic[tz]` to the pip requirements.
# string value is passed to ZoneInfo()
# leave blank for localtime
# timezone =

# max length of characters to apply to the "slug" field
# truncate_slug_length = 40

# set to 'true' to run the environment during
# the 'revision' command, regardless of autogenerate
# revision_environment = false

# set to 'true' to allow .pyc and .pyo files without
# a source .py file to be detected as revisions in the
# versions/ directory
# sourceless = false

# version location specification; This defaults
# to <script_location>/versions.  When using multiple version
# directories, initial revisions must be specified with --version-path.
# The path separator used here should be the separator specified by "path_separator"
# below.
# version_locations = %(here)s/bar:%(here)s/bat:%(here)s/alembic/versions

# path_separator; This indicates what character is used to split lists of file
# paths, including version_locations and prepend_sys_path within configparser
# files such as alembic.ini.
# The default rendered in new alembic.ini files is "os", which uses os.pathsep
# to provide os-dependent path splitting.
#
# Note that in order to support legacy alembic.ini files, this default does NOT
# take place if path_separator is not present in alembic.ini.  If this
# option is omitted entirely, fallback logic is as follows:
#
# 1. Parsing of the version_locations option falls back to using the legacy
#    "version_path_separator" key, which if absent then falls back to the legacy
#    behavior of splitting on spaces and/or commas.
# 2. Parsing of the prepend_sys_path option falls back to the legacy
#    behavior of splitting on spaces, commas, or colons.
#
# Valid values for path_separator are:
#
# path_separator = :
# path_separator = ;
# path_separator = space
# path_separator = newline
#
# Use os.pathsep. Default configuration used for new projects.
path_separator = os

# set to 'true' to search source files recursively
# in each "version_locations" directory
# new in Alembic version 1.10
# recursive_version_locations = false

# the output encoding used when revision files
# are written from script.py.mako
# output_encoding = utf-8

# database URL.  This is consumed by the user-maintained env.py script only.
# other means of configuring database URLs may be customized within the env.py
# file.
sqlalchemy.url = 


[post_write_hooks]
# post_write_hooks defines scripts or Python functions that are run
# on newly generated revision scripts.  See the documentation for further
# detail and examples

# format using "black" - use the console_scripts runner, against the "black" entrypoint
# hooks = black
# black.type = console_scripts
# black.entrypoint = black
# black.options = -l 79 REVISION_SCRIPT_FILENAME

# lint with attempts to fix using "ruff" - use the module runner, against the "ruff" module
# hooks = ruff
# ruff.type = module
# ruff.module = ruff
# ruff.options = check --fix REVISION_SCRIPT_FILENAME

# Alternatively, use the exec runner to execute a binary found on your PATH
# hooks = ruff
# ruff.type = exec
# ruff.executable = ruff
# ruff.options = check --fix REVISION_SCRIPT_FILENAME

# Logging configuration.  This is also consumed by the user-maintained
# env.py script only.
[loggers]
keys = root,sqlalchemy,alembic

[handlers]
keys = console

[formatters]
keys = generic

[logger_root]
level = WARNING
handlers = console
qualname =

[logger_sqlalchemy]
level = WARNING
handlers =
qualname = sqlalchemy.engine

[logger_alembic]
level = INFO
handlers =
qualname = alembic

[handler_console]
class = StreamHandler
args = (sys.stderr,)
level = NOTSET
formatter = generic

[formatter_generic]
format = %(levelname)-5.5s [%(name)s] %(message)s
datefmt = %H:%M:%S
```

## File: `docker-compose.yml`

**Size:** 583 bytes | **Lines included:** 44 | **Secret values redacted:** 1

```yaml
services:

  api:
    build: .

    # user: "${UID}:${GID}"

    container_name: fastapi-api

    security_opt:
      - seccomp:unconfined

    ports:
      - "8000:8000"

    volumes:
      - .:/app

    env_file:
      - .env

    depends_on:
      - postgres

  postgres:
    image: postgres:16

    container_name: fastapi-postgres

    restart: always

    environment:
      POSTGRES_USER: admin
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: [REDACTED]
      POSTGRES_DB: fastapi_db

    ports:
      - "5433:5432"

    volumes:
      - postgres_data:/var/lib/postgresql/data

volumes:
  postgres_data:
```

## File: `docs/diary/README.md`

**Size:** 9,424 bytes | **Lines included:** 234 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

````markdown
# FastAPI User Management — Development Diary

**Last updated:** 2026-11-01  
**Evidence baseline:** `project_context.md` export reviewed on 2026-11-01

This directory records the learning and development history of the FastAPI User Management project.

The diary is a historical engineering record:

- original entries describe what was recorded at the time;
- historical entries are not silently rewritten to match later architecture;
- retrospective notes are explicitly labelled;
- missing daily records remain visible as documentation gaps;
- source-code evidence is separated from test-source evidence, runtime verification, and README-only claims.

---

## Current project status — 2026-11-01

### Backend and database

The source export contains a FastAPI application using PostgreSQL, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, JWT authentication, and a Router → Service → Repository structure.

The migration chain represented in source is:

```text
e7d9b8aad922
      ↓
b31f9c2a6d10
      ↓
c42a7f91d8e3
```

The current models and migrations include:

- User roles `user` and `admin`, with a database CHECK constraint;
- User lifecycle fields `is_active`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `last_login_at`;
- Post lifecycle fields `status`, `created_at`, `updated_at`;
- allowed Post statuses `draft`, `published`, `archived`;
- pagination, search, authentication, authorization, and Post ownership checks.

FastAPI feature development was recorded as frozen at the 2026-10-18 milestone. This audit does not reopen backend scope.

### Testing

The 2026-10-18 diary records a full regression result of **79 passed**. This is a historical result, not a fresh test run.

A static scan of the current exported test source finds **83 test-function definitions**:

| Test area | Definitions |
|---|---:|
| API | 54 |
| Integration | 11 |
| E2E | 9 |
| Unit | 9 |
| **Total** | **83** |

A static function count is not a pytest collection result and does not mean 83 tests pass. The audit environment could not collect tests because `python-jose` (`jose`) was not installed. Python syntax compilation completed successfully.

### Python Analytics

The source export contains:

- `analytics/user_analysis.py`
- `analytics/post_analysis.py`
- `analytics/charts.py`

The current Analytics API is:

```text
GET /analytics/users/summary
```

It is admin-protected and returns total users and users grouped by role. The API tests cover unauthenticated access (401), a normal user (403), and an admin (200).

The Post Analytics helpers and chart helpers exist in source, but they are not exposed as separate API endpoints and have no dedicated analytics-helper test module in the export.

Important limitations found during review:

- the User DataFrame query loads the complete User ORM entity, including `password`, although the DataFrame output omits that field;
- the Post DataFrame omits lifecycle fields and returns a zero-column DataFrame when there are no Posts;
- Post helper functions fail on that empty DataFrame because `user_id` is missing;
- `calculate_post_statistics()` averages only across users with at least one Post, not across every User;
- the age chart requires an `age` column, which the current User model does not define.

See [Documentation Audit — 2026-11-01](./2026-11-01-documentation-audit.md).

### SQL analytics and Dashboard

The root README documents SQL analytics, KPI 7, a Next.js dashboard, JSON snapshots, a synchronization script, and a Vercel URL.

Those are **README-documented claims**, not independently verified source components in this export. The reason is the current `export_project.py` allowlist: it excludes `.sql`, `.json`, `.js`, `.mjs`, `.ts`, and `.tsx` files, among others.

The README documents a KPI snapshot with 600 users, 2,250 posts, 870 posts attributed to the top 10% creator group, and a 38.67% share, with a stated verification date of 2026-10-25. The arithmetic `870 / 2250 × 100 = 38.67%` is correct, but the SQL query and JSON snapshot were not included for independent validation.

The README also says the dashboard is snapshot-based and does not query PostgreSQL live. The full Next.js source and package manifest were not included in this export, so dashboard implementation and deployment were not re-tested.
````

````markdown
## 📖 Overview

**FastAPI User Management & Analytics Dashboard** is a project focused on backend development, database management, SQL analytics, and data visualization.

The project combines a FastAPI backend with PostgreSQL and a Next.js dashboard. The backend and SQL analytics support data processing and analysis, while the dashboard presents project progress and analytics metrics through a web interface.

The dashboard is deployed on Vercel and can be accessed online:

<p align="center">
  <a href="https://fastapi-user-management-dashboard-three.vercel.app">
    <strong>🚀 Open the Live Dashboard</strong>
  </a>
</p>

The project currently has two distinct parts:

- **Backend:** FastAPI and PostgreSQL, used for backend development and database operations.
- **Dashboard:** Next.js, used to present project progress and analytics information.

> **Important:** The dashboard is deployed online, but its analytics are generated from project files and JSON snapshots. It is not currently a live dashboard directly querying the FastAPI backend or PostgreSQL database. The displayed figures represent the available snapshot data, not guaranteed real-time database values.

---

## ✨ Features

### 🔹 Backend Development

- Build REST API functionality using FastAPI.
- Work with PostgreSQL for relational data storage.
- Run the database locally with Docker Compose.
- Access interactive API documentation through Swagger UI when the backend is running.
- Organize SQL analytics queries for data exploration and reporting.

### 🔹 SQL Analytics

- Analyze user and post data using SQL.
- Calculate overall data metrics.
- Evaluate the contribution of top creators to total post volume.
- Store verified KPI values in a dedicated JSON snapshot.
- Validate snapshot values against the analytics totals during dashboard data generation.

### 🔹 Analytics Dashboard

- Present project progress and summary statistics.
- Display user and post metrics.
- Visualize analytics data with charts.
- Generate progress data from project diaries, SQL analytics files, and JSON snapshots.
- Calculate the top-creator post share from verified KPI snapshot values.
- Build the dashboard automatically through the Next.js build process.

### 🔹 Online Deployment

- Deploy the Next.js dashboard to Vercel.
- Access the dashboard through a public URL.
- Maintain the backend and dashboard as separate components.
- Build and validate the dashboard locally before deployment.

---

| Category | Technology |
|---|---|
| Backend | Python, FastAPI |
| Database | PostgreSQL |
| Database Environment | Docker Compose |
| Query Language | SQL |
| Frontend Framework | Next.js |
| UI Library | React |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS v4 |
| Charts | Recharts |
| Data Pipeline | Node.js scripts and JSON snapshots |
| Frontend Deployment | Vercel |
| Version Control | Git and GitHub |

## 🏗️ Architecture

The project separates backend services, analytics resources, and dashboard presentation.

```text
                         ┌──────────────────────────┐
                         │        GitHub Repo       │
                         │  Source Code & SQL Files │
                         └────────────┬─────────────┘
                                      │
                 ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                 │                                         │
                 ▼                                         ▼
       ┌───────────────────┐                    ┌────────────────────┐
       │   FastAPI Backend │                    │   Analytics Files  │
       │                   │                    │                    │
       │   REST API        │                    │   SQL Queries      │
       │   Python          │                    │   Project Diaries  │
       └─────────┬─────────┘                    │   JSON Snapshots   │
                 │                              └─────────┬──────────┘
                 ▼                                        │
       ┌───────────────────┐                              ▼
       │    PostgreSQL     │                    ┌────────────────────┐
       │                   │                    │  Data Sync Script  │
       │    Database       │                    │ sync-progress.mjs  │
       └───────────────────┘                    └─────────┬──────────┘
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                ┌────────────────────┐
                                                │    Next.js Build   │
                                                │                    │
                                                │ Generated Progress │
                                                │ Analytics Dashboard│
                                                └─────────┬──────────┘
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                ┌────────────────────┐
                                                │       Vercel       │
                                                │   Online Dashboard │
                                                └────────────────────┘
```

### How the data flow works

1. The backend uses PostgreSQL for database operations.
2. SQL files contain queries used for analytics.
3. Project diaries and JSON snapshots provide the source data for the dashboard's progress and KPI summaries.
4. The `sync-progress.mjs` script reads and validates the available sources.
5. The script generates `progress.generated.json` for the dashboard.
6. The Next.js build process generates the production dashboard.
7. Vercel hosts the deployed frontend.

The current data pipeline generates dashboard data from project files. It does not automatically execute SQL against the production database or establish a live connection between Vercel and PostgreSQL.

---

**Notes:**

## 🚀 Getting Started

### 1. Clone the Repository

```bash
git clone https://github.com/Paoying5/fastapi-user-management.git
cd fastapi-user-management
```

### 2. Set Up PostgreSQL

The project uses Docker Compose to run PostgreSQL locally.

Start the PostgreSQL service:

```bash
docker compose up -d postgres
```

Check the running services:

```bash
docker compose ps
```

Connect to the database:

```bash
docker compose exec postgres psql -U admin -d fastapi_db
```

To exit the PostgreSQL command-line interface:

```sql
\q
```

The database container is configured to expose PostgreSQL on host port `5433`, mapped to container port `5432`.

Use the connection settings defined by your local Docker Compose and environment configuration when connecting from a local application.

### 3. Run the FastAPI Backend

Create a Python virtual environment from the repository root:

```bash
python -m venv .venv
```

Activate it on Windows:

```powershell
.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
```

Activate it on macOS or Linux:

```bash
source .venv/bin/activate
```

Install the backend dependencies using the dependency file provided by the project:

```bash
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

Start the FastAPI application using the entry point configured in the backend. For example, if the FastAPI instance is named `app` in the root-level `main.py` file:

```bash
uvicorn main:app --reload
```

Once the backend is running, open the interactive API documentation:

- **Swagger UI:** [http://localhost:8000/docs](http://localhost:8000/docs)
- **ReDoc:** [http://localhost:8000/redoc](http://localhost:8000/redoc)

> If your FastAPI entry point or dependency file is located elsewhere, use the path and command defined by the existing backend structure.

### 4. Run the Dashboard Locally

Open a new terminal and move to the dashboard directory:

```bash
cd mentor-web
```

Install the frontend dependencies:

```bash
npm install
```

Run the data synchronization script:

```bash
npm run sync-progress
```

Start the development server:

```bash
npm run dev
```

Open the dashboard in your browser:

[http://localhost:3000](http://localhost:3000)

#### Validate the Dashboard

Run the linter:

```bash
npm run lint
```

Build the production version:

```bash
npm run build
```

The build process runs the configured prebuild step, which synchronizes the dashboard data before compiling the Next.js application.

You can also check for whitespace errors in Git changes from the repository root:

```bash
git diff --check
```

---

## 📊 Analytics and Data Pipeline

The dashboard uses a data synchronization process to combine project progress information and verified analytics values.

### Data Sources

| Source | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `docs/diary/` | Project progress and development records |
| `sql/analytics/` | SQL analytics queries |
| `monthly-posts.snapshot.json` | Monthly post data used by the dashboard |
| `analytics-kpi.snapshot.json` | Verified KPI values used for analytics calculations |
| `sync-progress.mjs` | Reads, validates, and generates dashboard data |
| `progress.generated.json` | Generated dashboard progress data |

### KPI Snapshot

The file `mentor-web/src/data/analytics-kpi.snapshot.json` stores the verified values used for the top-creator KPI.

Current snapshot:

| Metric | Value |
|---|---:|
| Total users | 600 |
| Total posts | 2,250 |
| Posts attributed to the top 10% creator group | 870 |
| Post share of the top 10% creator group | 38.67% |

The KPI snapshot identifies its source as:

```text
sql/analytics/03_kpi_analysis.sql - KPI 7
```

The snapshot's verification date is `2026-10-25`.

### How the KPI Is Calculated

The dashboard calculates the top-creator post share from the snapshot values:

\[
\text{Top Creator Share}
=
\frac{\text{Top 10\% Posts}}{\text{All Posts}}
\times 100
\]

Using the current snapshot:

\[
\frac{870}{2250}\times 100 = 38.67\%
\]

The calculation is performed by the synchronization script rather than relying on a separately hardcoded percentage.

### Data Validation

The `sync-progress.mjs` script validates the KPI snapshot before generating dashboard data.

The validation process checks that:

- Required KPI fields are available.
- The snapshot's user total matches the extracted analytics user total.
- The snapshot's post total matches the extracted analytics post total.
- The top-creator share is calculated from the validated post counts.

If a required value or consistency check fails, the synchronization process should report the problem rather than silently generating a misleading KPI.

### Regenerate the Dashboard Data

From the `mentor-web/` directory, run:

```bash
npm run sync-progress
```

Then validate and build the dashboard:

```bash
npm run lint
npm run build
```

**Important:** The generated file `progress.generated.json` should not be edited manually. Update the relevant source data or snapshot, then run the synchronization script again.

The snapshot values should be refreshed and reverified whenever the underlying analytics data changes. The dashboard does not automatically retrieve updated PostgreSQL records simply because the site is deployed on Vercel.

---

## 🌐 Deployment

### Dashboard Deployment — Vercel

The Next.js dashboard is deployed online on Vercel.

<p align="center">
  <a href="https://fastapi-user-management-dashboard-three.vercel.app">
    <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Visit%20Dashboard-paoying--data.vercel.app-black?style=for-the-badge&logo=vercel" alt="Visit the online dashboard">
  </a>
</p>

**Live URL:** [https://fastapi-user-management-dashboard-three.vercel.app](https://fastapi-user-management-dashboard-three.vercel.app)

The dashboard source code is located in the `mentor-web/` directory.

For a Vercel project connected to this repository, use the following configuration:

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Framework Preset | Next.js |
| Root Directory | `mentor-web` |
| Install Command | `npm install` |
| Build Command | `npm run build` |

The build process uses the configured prebuild script to synchronize the progress data before creating the production build.

After deployment, open the live URL to verify that the dashboard loads and displays its available analytics data correctly.

### Backend Deployment

The online dashboard and FastAPI backend are separate components.

The dashboard's Vercel URL is **not** a public FastAPI API URL. A public backend endpoint should only be documented here after the FastAPI service has been deployed and its URL verified.

For local backend development, use the setup instructions in the [Getting Started](#-getting-started) section.

---

## 🔒 Security Notes

Follow these practices when developing or deploying the project:

- Do not commit `.env` files, passwords, database URLs, API keys, or other secrets.
- Keep local database credentials separate from frontend configuration.
- Do not expose PostgreSQL credentials to browser-side code.
- Do not place private database credentials in `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` environment variables.
- Do not assume that a static analytics snapshot represents real-time database data.
- Validate and refresh snapshots before publishing updated analytics.
- Use environment variables and the deployment platform's secret-management features for sensitive configuration.

The frontend should consume only data that is appropriate for public display.

---

## 🎯 Project Goals

This project supports practical learning and development in several areas:

- **Backend development:** Building and running a FastAPI application.
- **Database management:** Working with PostgreSQL and Docker Compose.
- **SQL analytics:** Writing and validating queries for user and post metrics.
- **Data processing:** Transforming source files and snapshots into dashboard-ready data.
- **Frontend development:** Building a Next.js dashboard with React, Tailwind CSS, and Recharts.
- **Data visualization:** Presenting metrics and project progress in a web interface.
- **Deployment:** Publishing the dashboard to Vercel.
- **Code quality:** Validating data, running lint checks, and testing production builds.

The project can be extended in the future to connect the dashboard to a deployed backend API, enabling analytics to be retrieved dynamically rather than from static snapshots.
````

## File: `analytics/charts.py`

**Size:** 2,196 bytes | **Lines included:** 113 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

```python
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import pandas as pd
from matplotlib.figure import Figure

from analytics.post_analysis import top_users_by_posts


def plot_users_by_role(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> Figure:
    """
    Create a bar chart showing the number of users for each role.
    """
    if "role" not in df.columns:
        raise ValueError(
            "DataFrame must contain a 'role' column"
        )

    counts = (
        df["role"]
        .fillna("unknown")
        .value_counts()
    )

    fig, ax = plt.subplots()

    ax.bar(
        counts.index,
        counts.values,
    )

    ax.set_title("Number of Users by Role")
    ax.set_xlabel("Role")
    ax.set_ylabel("Number of Users")

    fig.tight_layout()

    return fig


def plot_age_distribution(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> Figure:
    """
    Create an age histogram from a DataFrame that already contains
    an 'age' column.

    The current User database model does not provide age data,
    so this helper is not used by the FastAPI analytics endpoint.
    """
    if "age" not in df.columns:
        raise ValueError(
            "DataFrame must contain an 'age' column"
        )

    ages = df["age"].dropna()

    fig, ax = plt.subplots()

    ax.hist(
        ages,
        bins=10,
    )

    ax.set_title("User Age Distribution")
    ax.set_xlabel("Age")
    ax.set_ylabel("Number of Users")

    fig.tight_layout()

    return fig


def plot_top_users_by_posts(
    users_df: pd.DataFrame,
    posts_df: pd.DataFrame,
    limit: int = 10,
) -> Figure:
    """
    Create a bar chart of users with the highest number of posts.
    """
    if limit < 1:
        raise ValueError(
            "limit must be greater than or equal to 1"
        )

    top_users = top_users_by_posts(
        users_df=users_df,
        posts_df=posts_df,
        limit=limit,
    )

    fig, ax = plt.subplots()

    ax.bar(
        top_users["name"],
        top_users["post_count"],
    )

    ax.set_title(
        "Top Users by Number of Posts"
    )
    ax.set_xlabel("User")
    ax.set_ylabel("Number of Posts")

    ax.tick_params(
        axis="x",
        labelrotation=45,
    )

    fig.tight_layout()

    return fig
```

## File: `analytics/post_analysis.py`

**Size:** 2,466 bytes | **Lines included:** 131 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

```python
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from sqlalchemy import select

from app.models.post import Post


def get_posts_dataframe(session):
    posts = session.scalars(
        select(Post)
    ).all()

    data = []

    for post in posts:
        data.append(
            {
                "id": post.id,
                "title": post.title,
                "content": post.content,
                "user_id": post.user_id,
            }
        )

    return pd.DataFrame(data)

# Đếm số Post của từng User
def posts_per_user(df: pd.DataFrame):
    return (
        df["user_id"]
        .value_counts()
        .rename_axis("user_id")
        .reset_index(name="post_count")
    )
# tính thống kê Post

def calculate_post_statistics(df: pd.DataFrame):
    counts = (
        df["user_id"]
        .value_counts()
        .to_numpy()
    )

    if len(counts) == 0:
        return {
            "total_posts": 0,
            "average_posts_per_user": 0,
            "max_posts_by_one_user": 0,
            "min_posts_by_one_user": 0,
            "std": 0,
        }

    return {
        "total_posts": int(len(df)),
        "average_posts_per_user": float(
            np.mean(counts)
        ),
        "max_posts_by_one_user": int(
            np.max(counts)
        ),
        "min_posts_by_one_user": int(
            np.min(counts)
        ),
        "std": float(
            np.std(counts)
        ),
    }


def merge_users_posts(
    users_df: pd.DataFrame,
    posts_df: pd.DataFrame,
):
    post_counts = (
        posts_df["user_id"]
        .value_counts()
        .rename_axis("id")
        .reset_index(name="post_count")
    )

    result = users_df.merge(
        post_counts,
        on="id",
        how="left",
    )

    result["post_count"] = (
        result["post_count"]
        .fillna(0)
        .astype(int)
    )

    return result


def top_users_by_posts(
    users_df: pd.DataFrame,
    posts_df: pd.DataFrame,
    limit: int = 10,
):
    merged = merge_users_posts(
        users_df,
        posts_df,
    )

    return (
        merged
        .sort_values(
            "post_count",
            ascending=False,
        )
        .head(limit)
    )


def posts_by_role(
    users_df: pd.DataFrame,
    posts_df: pd.DataFrame,
):
    merged = merge_users_posts(
        users_df,
        posts_df,
    )

    return (
        merged
        .groupby("role")["post_count"]
        .sum()
        .reset_index()
    )
```

## File: `analytics/user_analysis.py`

**Size:** 1,464 bytes | **Lines included:** 75 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

```python
import pandas as pd
from sqlalchemy import select
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.models.user import User


def get_users_dataframe(
    session: Session,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Load the currently supported User fields into a DataFrame.

    This function intentionally uses only columns that actually exist
    in the current SQLAlchemy User model.
    """
    users = session.scalars(
        select(User)
    ).all()

    data = [
        {
            "id": user.id,
            "name": user.name,
            "email": user.email,
            "role": user.role,
            "full_name": user.full_name,
        }
        for user in users
    ]

    return pd.DataFrame(
        data,
        columns=[
            "id",
            "name",
            "email",
            "role",
            "full_name",
        ],
    )


def count_users(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> int:
    return len(df)


def users_by_role(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Count users grouped by role.

    Null roles are grouped as 'unknown' so analytics does not silently
    discard those records.
    """
    if df.empty:
        return pd.DataFrame(
            columns=[
                "role",
                "user_count",
            ]
        )

    role_counts = (
        df["role"]
        .fillna("unknown")
        .value_counts()
        .rename_axis("role")
        .reset_index(name="user_count")
    )

    return role_counts
```

## File: `app/database.py`

**Size:** 680 bytes | **Lines included:** 35 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

```python
from collections.abc import Generator

from sqlalchemy import create_engine
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session, declarative_base, sessionmaker

from app.core.config import settings


engine = create_engine(
    settings.DATABASE_URL,
    pool_pre_ping=True,
)

SessionLocal = sessionmaker(
    autocommit=False,
    autoflush=False,
    bind=engine,
)

Base = declarative_base()


def get_db() -> Generator[Session, None, None]:
    """
    Create one SQLAlchemy Session for one request.

    The session is always closed after the request finishes,
    including when an exception occurs.
    """
    db = SessionLocal()

    try:
        yield db
    finally:
        db.close()
```

```python
from fastapi import (
    FastAPI,
    Request,
    status,
)
from fastapi.responses import JSONResponse

from app.core.exceptions import (
    ConflictError,
    PermissionDeniedError,
    ResourceNotFoundError,
)
from app.routers import (
    analytics,
    auth,
    health,
    post,
    user,
)


app = FastAPI(
    title="FastAPI User Management",
    version="0.2.0",
    description=(
        "User and post management API built with "
        "FastAPI, SQLAlchemy, PostgreSQL and JWT."
    ),
)


@app.exception_handler(
    ResourceNotFoundError
)
async def resource_not_found_handler(
    request: Request,
    exc: ResourceNotFoundError,
):
    return JSONResponse(
        status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND,
        content={
            "detail": exc.message,
        },
    )


@app.exception_handler(
    PermissionDeniedError
)
async def permission_denied_handler(
    request: Request,
    exc: PermissionDeniedError,
):
    return JSONResponse(
        status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN,
        content={
            "detail": exc.message,
        },
    )


@app.exception_handler(
    ConflictError
)
async def conflict_handler(
    request: Request,
    exc: ConflictError,
):
    return JSONResponse(
        status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT,
        content={
            "detail": exc.message,
        },
    )


app.include_router(
    health.router
)

app.include_router(
    auth.router
)

app.include_router(
    user.router
)

app.include_router(
    post.router
)

app.include_router(
    analytics.router
)


@app.get(
    "/",
    tags=["Default"],
)
def root():
    return {
        "message": (
            "FastAPI User Management is running"
        ),
        "docs": "/docs",
        "health": "/health",
    }
```

## File: `app/repositories/post_repository.py`

**Size:** 3,001 bytes | **Lines included:** 163 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

```python
from sqlalchemy import (
    desc,
    func,
    select,
)
from sqlalchemy.orm import (
    Session,
    joinedload,
)

from app.models.post import Post


class PostRepository:
    """Persistence operations for Post entities."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        db: Session,
    ):
        self.db = db

    def get_all(
        self,
        limit: int = 10,
        offset: int = 0,
        search: str = "",
    ) -> list[Post]:
        statement = (
            select(Post)
            .options(
                joinedload(
                    Post.owner
                )
            )
        )

        if search:
            statement = (
                statement.where(
                    Post.title.ilike(
                        f"%{search}%"
                    )
                )
            )

        statement = (
            statement
            .order_by(
                desc(Post.id)
            )
            .offset(offset)
            .limit(limit)
        )

        return list(
            self.db.scalars(
                statement
            ).all()
        )

    def count(
        self,
        search: str = "",
    ) -> int:
        statement = (
            select(
                func.count()
            )
            .select_from(Post)
        )

        if search:
            statement = (
                statement.where(
                    Post.title.ilike(
                        f"%{search}%"
                    )
                )
            )

        result = self.db.scalar(
            statement
        )

        return int(
            result or 0
        )

    def get_by_id(
        self,
        post_id: int,
    ) -> Post | None:
        statement = (
            select(Post)
            .options(
                joinedload(
                    Post.owner
                )
            )
            .where(
                Post.id
                == post_id
            )
        )

        return (
            self.db
            .scalars(statement)
            .first()
        )

    def get_by_user_id(
        self,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> list[Post]:
        statement = (
            select(Post)
            .options(
                joinedload(
                    Post.owner
                )
            )
            .where(
                Post.user_id
                == user_id
            )
            .order_by(
                desc(Post.id)
            )
        )

        return list(
            self.db.scalars(
                statement
            ).all()
        )

    def add(
        self,
        post: Post,
    ) -> Post:
        self.db.add(post)
        self.db.flush()
        self.db.refresh(post)

        return post

    def update(
        self,
        post: Post,
    ) -> Post:
        self.db.flush()
        self.db.refresh(post)

        return post

    def delete(
        self,
        post: Post,
    ) -> None:
        self.db.delete(post)
        self.db.flush()
```

## File: `app/repositories/user_repository.py`

**Size:** 3,258 bytes | **Lines included:** 174 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

```python
from sqlalchemy import (
    asc,
    func,
    or_,
    select,
)
from sqlalchemy.orm import (
    Session,
    joinedload,
    selectinload,
)

from app.models.user import User


class UserRepository:
    def __init__(
        self,
        db: Session,
    ):
        self.db = db

    def get_all(
        self,
        limit: int = 10,
        offset: int = 0,
        search: str = "",
    ) -> list[User]:
        statement = (
            select(User)
            .options(
                selectinload(
                    User.posts
                )
            )
        )

        if search:
            pattern = (
                f"%{search}%"
            )

            statement = (
                statement.where(
                    or_(
                        User.name.ilike(
                            pattern
                        ),
                        User.email.ilike(
                            pattern
                        ),
                    )
                )
            )

        statement = (
            statement
            .order_by(
                asc(User.id)
            )
            .offset(offset)
            .limit(limit)
        )

        return list(
            self.db.scalars(
                statement
            ).all()
        )

    def count(
        self,
        search: str = "",
    ) -> int:
        statement = (
            select(
                func.count()
            )
            .select_from(User)
        )

        if search:
            pattern = (
                f"%{search}%"
            )

            statement = (
                statement.where(
                    or_(
                        User.name.ilike(
                            pattern
                        ),
                        User.email.ilike(
                            pattern
                        ),
                    )
                )
            )

        result = self.db.scalar(
            statement
        )

        return int(
            result or 0
        )

    def get_by_id(
        self,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> User | None:
        statement = (
            select(User)
            .options(
                joinedload(
                    User.posts
                )
            )
            .where(
                User.id
                == user_id
            )
        )

        return (
            self.db
            .scalars(statement)
            .unique()
            .first()
        )

    def get_by_email(
        self,
        email: str,
    ) -> User | None:
        statement = (
            select(User)
            .where(
                User.email
                == email
            )
        )

        return (
            self.db
            .scalars(statement)
            .first()
        )

    def add(
        self,
        user: User,
    ) -> User:
        self.db.add(user)
        self.db.flush()
        self.db.refresh(user)

        return user

    def update(
        self,
        user: User,
    ) -> User:
        self.db.flush()
        self.db.refresh(user)

        return user

    def delete(
        self,
        user: User,
    ) -> None:
        self.db.delete(user)
        self.db.flush()
```

```python
from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from analytics.user_analysis import (
    count_users,
    get_users_dataframe,
    users_by_role,
)
from app.dependencies import (
    get_admin_user,
    get_db,
)
from app.models import User
from app.schemas import APIResponse
from app.utils.response import response


router = APIRouter(
    prefix="/analytics",
    tags=["Analytics"],
)


@router.get(
    "/users/summary",
    response_model=APIResponse,
    summary="Get user analytics summary",
)
def user_summary(
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    admin: User = Depends(get_admin_user),
) -> dict:
    """
    Return basic user statistics.

    Access is restricted to admin users.
    """
    users_df = get_users_dataframe(db)

    role_counts = users_by_role(
        users_df
    )

    data = {
        "total_users": count_users(
            users_df
        ),
        "users_by_role": role_counts.to_dict(
            orient="records"
        ),
    }

    return response(
        "User analytics retrieved successfully",
        data,
    )
```

```python
from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, status
from sqlalchemy import text
from sqlalchemy.exc import SQLAlchemyError
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.dependencies import get_db


router = APIRouter(
    tags=["Health"],
)


@router.get("/health")
def health_check(
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
):
    try:
        db.execute(
            text("SELECT 1"),
        )

    except SQLAlchemyError as exc:
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
            detail="Database connection failed",
        ) from exc

    return {
        "status": "healthy",
        "api": "running",
        "database": "connected",
    }
```

```python
    PAGINATION_HEADERS_OPENAPI,
```

```python
)
from app.utils.response import response


router = APIRouter(
    prefix="/users",
    tags=["Users"],
)


@router.get(
    "/",
    response_model=APIResponse[
        list[UserResponse]
    ],
    summary="Get all users",
    responses={
        200: {
            "description": (
                "Users retrieved successfully"
            ),
            "headers":
                PAGINATION_HEADERS_OPENAPI,
        },
    },
)
def get_users(
    http_response: Response,
    limit: int = Query(
        default=10,
        ge=1,
        le=100,
        description=(
            "Số lượng bản ghi tối đa trả về"
        ),
    ),
    offset: int = Query(
        default=0,
        ge=0,
        description=(
            "Số lượng bản ghi bỏ qua"
        ),
    ),
    search: str = Query(
        default="",
        max_length=100,
        description=(
            "Tìm kiếm theo tên hoặc email"
        ),
    ),
    service: UserService = Depends(
        get_user_service
    ),
    _admin: User = Depends(
        get_admin_user
    ),
):
    users, total = (
        service.get_users(
            limit=limit,
            offset=offset,
            search=search,
        )
    )

    http_response.headers.update(
        build_pagination_headers(
            total=total,
            limit=limit,
            offset=offset,
        )
    )

    data = [
        UserResponse
        .model_validate(user)
        .model_dump()
        for user in users
    ]

    return response(
        "Users retrieved successfully",
        data,
    )

    response_model=APIResponse[

    ],

)
def get_user(

    ),
    _admin: User = Depends(

    ),
):
    user = service.get_user(

    )

    data = (
        UserResponse
        .model_validate(user)
        .model_dump()
    )

    return response(
        "User found",
        data,
    )

        UserResponse
    ],

    summary="Create user",
)
def create_user(

    ),
):
    db_user = service.create_user(

    )

    data = (

        .model_validate(db_user)
        .model_dump()
    )

    return response(

    )

    ],

)
def update_user(

    ),

    ],

):

        .model_validate(

        )
        .model_dump()
    )

    return response(
        "User partially updated successfully",
        data,
    )


@router.delete(
    "/{user_id}",
    response_model=APIResponse[
        None
    ],
    summary="Delete user",
)
def delete_user(
    user_id: int,
    service: UserService = Depends(
        get_user_service
    ),
    _admin: User = Depends(
        get_admin_user
    ),

    )

    )
```

```python
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Annotated, Literal

from pydantic import (
    BaseModel,
    ConfigDict,
    StringConstraints,
    field_validator,
)


PostTitle = Annotated[
    str,
    StringConstraints(
        strip_whitespace=True,
        min_length=2,
        max_length=255,
    ),
]


PostStatus = Literal[
    "draft",
    "published",
    "archived",
]


class StrictRequestModel(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(
        extra="forbid",
    )


class PostCreate(StrictRequestModel):
    title: PostTitle
    content: str | None = None
    status: PostStatus = "published"


class PostUpdate(StrictRequestModel):
    title: PostTitle
    content: str | None = None


class PostPatch(StrictRequestModel):
    title: PostTitle | None = None
    content: str | None = None
    status: PostStatus | None = None

    @field_validator("title")
    @classmethod
    def title_cannot_be_null(
        cls,
        value,
    ):
        if value is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "title cannot be null"
            )

        return value

    @field_validator("status")
    @classmethod
    def status_cannot_be_null(
        cls,
        value,
    ):
        if value is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "status cannot be null"
            )

        return value


class PostSimple(BaseModel):
    id: int
    title: str

    model_config = ConfigDict(
        from_attributes=True,
    )


class UserSimple(BaseModel):
    id: int
    name: str
    email: str

    model_config = ConfigDict(
        from_attributes=True,
    )


class PostResponse(BaseModel):
    id: int
    title: str
    content: str | None
    user_id: int

    status: PostStatus
    created_at: datetime
    updated_at: datetime

    owner: UserSimple

    model_config = ConfigDict(
        from_attributes=True,
    )
```

## File: `app/services/auth_service.py`

**Size:** 1,979 bytes | **Lines included:** 101 | **Secret values redacted:** 4

```python
from datetime import datetime, timezone

from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.core.security import (
    create_access_token,
    verify_password,
)
from app.models.user import User
from app.repositories.user_repository import (
    UserRepository,
)
from app.schemas.auth import Token


class AuthService:
    """Authentication business rules."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        db: Session,
    ):
        self.db = db
        self.repository = UserRepository(
            db
        )

    def authenticate_user(
        self,
        email: str,
        password: [REDACTED],
    ) -> User | None:
        email = email.strip().lower()

        user = self.repository.get_by_email(
            email
        )

        if user is None:
            return None

        if not verify_password(
            password,
            user.password,
        ):
            return None

        if not user.is_active:
            return None

        return user

    def login(
        self,
        email: str,
        password: [REDACTED],
    ) -> Token | None:
        user = self.authenticate_user(
            email,
            password,
        )

        if user is None:
            return None

        try:
            user.last_login_at = datetime.now(
                timezone.utc
            )

            self.db.commit()
            self.db.refresh(user)

        except Exception:
            self.db.rollback()
            raise

        return Token(
            access_token=[REDACTED]
                {
                    "sub": str(user.id),
                }
            ),
            token_type="[REDACTED]",
        )

    def get_user_by_email(
        self,
        email: str,
    ) -> User | None:
        return self.repository.get_by_email(
            email.strip().lower()
        )

    def get_user_by_id(
        self,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> User | None:
        return self.repository.get_by_id(
            user_id
        )
```

```python
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.core.exceptions import (
    PermissionDeniedError,
    ResourceNotFoundError,
)
from app.models.post import Post
from app.repositories.post_repository import PostRepository
from app.schemas.post import (
    PostCreate,
    PostPatch,
    PostUpdate,
)


class PostService:
    """Business rules for posts, ownership and transactions."""

    def __init__(self, db: Session):
        self.db = db
        self.repository = PostRepository(db)

    def create_post(
        self,
        post_data: PostCreate,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> Post:
        post = Post(
            title=post_data.title,
            content=post_data.content,
            status=post_data.status,
            user_id=user_id,
        )

        try:
            post = self.repository.add(post)

            self.db.commit()

            created_post = self.repository.get_by_id(
                post.id
            )

            if created_post is None:
                raise ResourceNotFoundError(
                    "Post not found after creation"
                )

            return created_post

        except Exception:
            self.db.rollback()
            raise

    def get_posts(
        self,
        limit: int = 10,
        offset: int = 0,
        search: str = "",
    ) -> tuple[list[Post], int]:
        posts = (
            self.repository.get_all(
                limit=limit,
                offset=offset,
                search=search,
            )
        )

        total = (
            self.repository.count(
                search=search
            )
        )

        return posts, total
    
    def get_my_posts(
        self,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> list[Post]:
        return self.repository.get_by_user_id(
            user_id
        )

    def get_post(
        self,
        post_id: int,
    ) -> Post:
        post = self.repository.get_by_id(
            post_id
        )

        if post is None:
            raise ResourceNotFoundError(
                "Post not found"
            )

        return post

    def update_post(
        self,
        post_id: int,
        post_data: PostUpdate,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> Post:
        post = self._get_owned_post(
            post_id,
            user_id,
        )

        post.title = post_data.title
        post.content = post_data.content

        return self._commit_update(post)

    def patch_post(
        self,
        post_id: int,
        post_data: PostPatch,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> Post:
        post = self._get_owned_post(
            post_id,
            user_id,
        )

        update_data = post_data.model_dump(
            exclude_unset=True
        )

        for field_name, value in update_data.items():
            setattr(
                post,
                field_name,
                value,
            )

        return self._commit_update(post)

    def delete_post(
        self,
        post_id: int,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> None:
        post = self._get_owned_post(
            post_id,
            user_id,
        )

        try:
            self.repository.delete(post)
            self.db.commit()

        except Exception:
            self.db.rollback()
            raise

    def _get_owned_post(
        self,
        post_id: int,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> Post:
        post = self.repository.get_by_id(
            post_id
        )

        if post is None:
            raise ResourceNotFoundError(
                "Post not found"
            )

        if post.user_id != user_id:
            raise PermissionDeniedError(
                "You do not own this post"
            )

        return post

    def _commit_update(
        self,
        post: Post,
    ) -> Post:
        try:
            post = self.repository.update(post)

            self.db.commit()
            self.db.refresh(post)

            return post

        except Exception:
            self.db.rollback()
            raise
```

```python
from typing import NoReturn

from sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.core.exceptions import (
    ConflictError,
    ResourceNotFoundError,
)
from app.core.security import hash_password
from app.models.user import User
from app.repositories.user_repository import UserRepository
from app.schemas.user import (
    UserCreate,
    UserPatch,
    UserUpdate,
)


class UserService:
    def __init__(self, db: Session):
        self.db = db
        self.repository = UserRepository(db)

    def get_users(
        self,
        limit: int = 10,
        offset: int = 0,
        search: str = "",
    ) -> tuple[list[User], int]:
        users = (
            self.repository.get_all(
                limit=limit,
                offset=offset,
                search=search,
            )
        )

        total = (
            self.repository.count(
                search=search
            )
        )

        return users, total
    
    def get_user(
        self,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> User:
        user = self.repository.get_by_id(
            user_id
        )

        if user is None:
            raise ResourceNotFoundError(
                "User not found"
            )

        return user

    def create_user(
        self,
        user_data: UserCreate,
    ) -> User:
        email = self._normalize_email(
            str(user_data.email)
        )

        if (
            self.repository.get_by_email(email)
            is not None
        ):
            raise ConflictError(
                "Email already exists"
            )

        user = User(
            name=user_data.name,
            email=email,
            role="user",
            password=[REDACTED]
                user_data.password
            ),
            full_name=user_data.full_name,
        )

        try:
            user = self.repository.add(user)

            self.db.commit()
            self.db.refresh(user)

            return user

        except IntegrityError as exc:
            self._handle_integrity_error(exc)

        except Exception:
            self.db.rollback()
            raise

    def update_user(
        self,
        user_id: int,
        user_data: UserUpdate,
    ) -> User:
        user = self.repository.get_by_id(
            user_id
        )

        if user is None:
            raise ResourceNotFoundError(
                "User not found"
            )

        email = self._normalize_email(
            str(user_data.email)
        )

        if (
            user.email != email
            and self.repository.get_by_email(email)
            is not None
        ):
            raise ConflictError(
                "Email already exists"
            )

        user.name = user_data.name
        user.email = email
        user.role = user_data.role
        user.full_name = user_data.full_name

        return self._commit_update(user)

    def patch_user(
        self,
        user_id: int,
        user_data: UserPatch,
    ) -> User:
        user = self.repository.get_by_id(
            user_id
        )

        if user is None:
            raise ResourceNotFoundError(
                "User not found"
            )

        update_data = user_data.model_dump(
            exclude_unset=True
        )

        if (
            "email" in update_data
            and update_data["email"] is not None
        ):
            email = self._normalize_email(
                str(update_data["email"])
            )

            if (
                user.email != email
                and self.repository.get_by_email(
                    email
                )
                is not None
            ):
                raise ConflictError(
                    "Email already exists"
                )

            update_data["email"] = email

        for field_name, value in update_data.items():
            setattr(
                user,
                field_name,
                value,
            )

        return self._commit_update(user)

    def delete_user(
        self,
        user_id: int,
    ) -> None:
        user = self.repository.get_by_id(
            user_id
        )

        if user is None:
            raise ResourceNotFoundError(
                "User not found"
            )

        try:
            self.repository.delete(user)
            self.db.commit()

        except Exception:
            self.db.rollback()
            raise

    def _commit_update(
        self,
        user: User,
    ) -> User:
        try:
            user = self.repository.update(user)

            self.db.commit()
            self.db.refresh(user)

            return user

        except IntegrityError as exc:
            self._handle_integrity_error(exc)

        except Exception:
            self.db.rollback()
            raise

    @staticmethod
    def _normalize_email(
        email: str,
    ) -> str:
        return email.strip().lower()

    def _handle_integrity_error(
        self,
        exc: IntegrityError,
    ) -> NoReturn:
        self.db.rollback()

        original_error = str(
            exc.orig
        ).lower()

        diagnostic = getattr(
            exc.orig,
            "diag",
            None,
        )

        constraint_name = getattr(
            diagnostic,
            "constraint_name",
            None,
        )

        email_constraints = {
            "ix_users_email",
            "users_email_key",
        }

        is_email_conflict = (
            constraint_name in email_constraints
            or (
                "users.email" in original_error
                and (
                    "unique" in original_error
                    or "duplicate" in original_error
                )
            )
        )

        if is_email_conflict:
            raise ConflictError(
                "Email already exists"
            ) from exc

        raise exc
```

```sql
BEGIN;

-- Reproducible random values.
SELECT setseed(0.42);

-- =========================================================
-- CLEAN PREVIOUS ANALYTICS SEED ONLY
-- Không đụng vào test/dev data hiện tại.
-- =========================================================

DELETE FROM posts
WHERE user_id IN (
    SELECT id
    FROM users
    WHERE email LIKE 'analytics\_user\_%@example.com' ESCAPE '\'
);

DELETE FROM users
WHERE email LIKE 'analytics\_user\_%@example.com' ESCAPE '\';


-- =========================================================
-- SEED 600 ANALYTICS USERS
-- =========================================================

WITH password_source AS (
    SELECT password
    FROM users
    ORDER BY id
    LIMIT 1
),

generated_users AS (
    SELECT
        seq,

        NOW()
        - (
            random()
            * 300
            * 24
            * 60
            * 60
        ) * INTERVAL '1 second'
        AS created_at

    FROM generate_series(
        1,
        600
    ) AS seq
)

INSERT INTO users (
    name,
    email,
    role,
    password,
    full_name,
    is_active,
    created_at,
    updated_at,
    last_login_at
)

SELECT
    'Analytics User ' || seq,

    'analytics_user_'
        || LPAD(seq::text, 4, '0')
        || '@example.com',

    CASE
        WHEN seq % 20 = 0
            THEN 'admin'
        ELSE 'user'
    END,

    password_source.password,

    'Analytics User ' || seq,

    CASE
        WHEN seq % 10 = 0
            THEN false
        ELSE true
    END,

    created_at,

    LEAST(
        NOW(),

        created_at
        + (
            random()
            * EXTRACT(
                EPOCH FROM (
                    NOW() - created_at
                )
            )
        ) * INTERVAL '1 second'
    ),

    CASE
        WHEN seq % 7 = 0
            THEN NULL

        ELSE LEAST(
            NOW(),

            created_at
            + (
                random()
                * EXTRACT(
                    EPOCH FROM (
                        NOW() - created_at
                    )
                )
            ) * INTERVAL '1 second'
        )
    END

FROM generated_users
CROSS JOIN password_source;


-- =========================================================
-- CREATE USER BEHAVIOR SEGMENTS
--
-- mỗi nhóm 20 users:
-- 4 users  -> 0 posts
-- 5 users  -> 1 post
-- 7 users  -> 2–5 posts
-- 3 users  -> 6–10 posts
-- 1 user   -> 11–20 posts
-- =========================================================

WITH ranked_users AS (
    SELECT
        id,
        created_at,

        ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
            ORDER BY id
        ) AS rn

    FROM users

    WHERE email LIKE
        'analytics\_user\_%@example.com' ESCAPE '\'
),

user_post_counts AS (
    SELECT
        id,
        created_at,

        CASE
            WHEN rn % 20 IN (
                0, 1, 2, 3
            )
                THEN 0

            WHEN rn % 20 IN (
                4, 5, 6, 7, 8
            )
                THEN 1

            WHEN rn % 20 IN (
                9, 10, 11,
                12, 13, 14, 15
            )
                THEN 2 + (rn % 4)

            WHEN rn % 20 IN (
                16, 17, 18
            )
                THEN 6 + (rn % 5)

            ELSE
                11 + (rn % 10)
        END AS post_count

    FROM ranked_users
),

expanded_posts AS (
    SELECT
        u.id AS user_id,
        u.created_at AS user_created_at,
        gs.post_no

    FROM user_post_counts u

    JOIN LATERAL generate_series(
        1,
        u.post_count
    ) AS gs(post_no)
        ON true
),

prepared_posts AS (
    SELECT
        user_id,
        post_no,

        ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
            ORDER BY
                user_id,
                post_no
        ) AS post_seq,

        user_created_at
        + (
            random()
            * EXTRACT(
                EPOCH FROM (
                    NOW() - user_created_at
                )
            )
        ) * INTERVAL '1 second'
        AS post_created_at

    FROM expanded_posts
)

INSERT INTO posts (
    title,
    content,
    user_id,
    status,
    created_at,
    updated_at
)

SELECT
    'Analytics Post '
        || user_id
        || '-'
        || post_no,

    'Synthetic analytics content for user '
        || user_id
        || ', post '
        || post_no
        || '.',

    user_id,

    CASE
        WHEN post_seq % 10 = 0
            THEN 'archived'

        WHEN post_seq % 10 IN (
            1, 2
        )
            THEN 'draft'

        ELSE 'published'
    END,

    post_created_at,

    LEAST(
        NOW(),

        post_created_at
        + (
            random()
            * EXTRACT(
                EPOCH FROM (
                    NOW() - post_created_at
                )
            )
        ) * INTERVAL '1 second'
    )

FROM prepared_posts;


COMMIT;
```

**Size:** 5,707 bytes | **Lines included:** 311 | **Secret values redacted:** 0

```sql
-- =========================================================
-- DATA ANALYST PHASE 1
-- EXPLORATORY SQL ANALYSIS
-- =========================================================


-- =========================================================
-- 1. ANALYTICS USERS VIEW
-- =========================================================

CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW v_analytics_users AS
SELECT *
FROM users
WHERE email LIKE
    'analytics\_user\_%@example.com'
    ESCAPE '\';


-- =========================================================
-- 2. ANALYTICS POSTS VIEW
-- =========================================================

CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW v_analytics_posts AS
SELECT
    p.*
FROM posts p
JOIN v_analytics_users u
    ON u.id = p.user_id;


-- =========================================================
-- 3. BASIC DATASET SIZE
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    COUNT(*) AS total_users
FROM v_analytics_users;


SELECT
    COUNT(*) AS total_posts
FROM v_analytics_posts;


-- =========================================================
-- 4. CORE ENGAGEMENT KPIs
-- =========================================================

WITH user_post_counts AS (
    SELECT
        u.id,
        COUNT(p.id) AS post_count
    FROM v_analytics_users u
    LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
        ON p.user_id = u.id
    GROUP BY u.id
)

SELECT
    COUNT(*) AS total_users,

    COUNT(*) FILTER (
        WHERE post_count > 0
    ) AS posting_users,

    COUNT(*) FILTER (
        WHERE post_count = 0
    ) AS non_posting_users,

    ROUND(
        100.0
        * COUNT(*) FILTER (
            WHERE post_count > 0
        )
        / COUNT(*),
        2
    ) AS posting_rate_percent,

    ROUND(
        AVG(post_count),
        2
    ) AS avg_posts_per_user,

    ROUND(
        AVG(post_count) FILTER (
            WHERE post_count > 0
        ),
        2
    ) AS avg_posts_per_posting_user

FROM user_post_counts;


-- =========================================================
-- 5. POST STATUS DISTRIBUTION
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS total_posts,

    ROUND(
        100.0 * COUNT(*)
        / SUM(COUNT(*)) OVER (),
        2
    ) AS percentage

FROM v_analytics_posts

GROUP BY status

ORDER BY total_posts DESC;


-- =========================================================
-- 6. USER SEGMENTATION
-- =========================================================

WITH user_posts AS (
    SELECT
        u.id,
        COUNT(p.id) AS post_count

    FROM v_analytics_users u

    LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
        ON p.user_id = u.id

    GROUP BY u.id
),

segments AS (
    SELECT
        id,
        post_count,

        CASE
            WHEN post_count = 0
                THEN 'No posts'

            WHEN post_count = 1
                THEN '1 post'

            WHEN post_count BETWEEN 2 AND 5
                THEN '2-5 posts'

            WHEN post_count BETWEEN 6 AND 10
                THEN '6-10 posts'

            ELSE '11-20 posts'
        END AS user_segment

    FROM user_posts
)

SELECT
    user_segment,
    COUNT(*) AS total_users,

    ROUND(
        100.0 * COUNT(*)
        / SUM(COUNT(*)) OVER (),
        2
    ) AS percentage

FROM segments

GROUP BY user_segment

ORDER BY total_users DESC;


-- =========================================================
-- 7. ROLE DISTRIBUTION
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    role,
    COUNT(*) AS total_users,

    ROUND(
        100.0 * COUNT(*)
        / SUM(COUNT(*)) OVER (),
        2
    ) AS percentage

FROM v_analytics_users

GROUP BY role

ORDER BY total_users DESC;


-- =========================================================
-- 8. ACTIVE / INACTIVE DISTRIBUTION
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    is_active,
    COUNT(*) AS total_users,

    ROUND(
        100.0 * COUNT(*)
        / SUM(COUNT(*)) OVER (),
        2
    ) AS percentage

FROM v_analytics_users

GROUP BY is_active

ORDER BY is_active DESC;


-- =========================================================
-- 9. TOP 10 CONTENT CREATORS
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    u.id,
    u.name,
    u.role,
    u.is_active,
    COUNT(p.id) AS total_posts

FROM v_analytics_users u

LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
    ON p.user_id = u.id

GROUP BY
    u.id,
    u.name,
    u.role,
    u.is_active

ORDER BY
    total_posts DESC,
    u.id

LIMIT 10;


-- =========================================================
-- 10. MONTHLY USER REGISTRATIONS
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    DATE_TRUNC(
        'month',
        created_at
    )::date AS month,

    COUNT(*) AS new_users

FROM v_analytics_users

GROUP BY month

ORDER BY month;


-- =========================================================
-- 11. MONTHLY POST ACTIVITY
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    DATE_TRUNC(
        'month',
        created_at
    )::date AS month,

    COUNT(*) AS total_posts

FROM v_analytics_posts

GROUP BY month

ORDER BY month;


-- =========================================================
-- 12. ACTIVE VS INACTIVE ENGAGEMENT
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    u.is_active,

    COUNT(DISTINCT u.id)
        AS total_users,

    COUNT(p.id)
        AS total_posts,

    ROUND(
        COUNT(p.id)::numeric
        / NULLIF(
            COUNT(DISTINCT u.id),
            0
        ),
        2
    ) AS avg_posts_per_user

FROM v_analytics_users u

LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
    ON p.user_id = u.id

GROUP BY u.is_active

ORDER BY u.is_active DESC;
```

```sql
-- =========================================================
-- DATA ANALYST PHASE 2
-- BUSINESS KPI ANALYSIS
-- =========================================================


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 1
-- OVERALL PLATFORM ENGAGEMENT
-- =========================================================

WITH user_post_counts AS (
    SELECT
        u.id,
        COUNT(p.id) AS post_count

    FROM v_analytics_users u

    LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
        ON p.user_id = u.id

    GROUP BY u.id
)

SELECT
    COUNT(*) AS total_users,

    COUNT(*) FILTER (
        WHERE post_count > 0
    ) AS posting_users,

    ROUND(
        100.0
        * COUNT(*) FILTER (
            WHERE post_count > 0
        )
        / COUNT(*),
        2
    ) AS posting_rate_percent,

    ROUND(
        AVG(post_count),
        2
    ) AS avg_posts_per_user,

    MAX(post_count) AS max_posts_per_user

FROM user_post_counts;


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 2
-- ACTIVE VS INACTIVE USER ENGAGEMENT
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    u.is_active,

    COUNT(DISTINCT u.id)
        AS total_users,

    COUNT(p.id)
        AS total_posts,

    COUNT(DISTINCT p.user_id)
        AS posting_users,

    ROUND(
        100.0
        * COUNT(DISTINCT p.user_id)
        / COUNT(DISTINCT u.id),
        2
    ) AS posting_rate_percent,

    ROUND(
        COUNT(p.id)::numeric
        / NULLIF(
            COUNT(DISTINCT u.id),
            0
        ),
        2
    ) AS avg_posts_per_user

FROM v_analytics_users u

LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
    ON p.user_id = u.id

GROUP BY u.is_active

ORDER BY u.is_active DESC;


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 3
-- MONTHLY USER ACQUISITION + MOM GROWTH
-- =========================================================

WITH monthly_users AS (
    SELECT
        DATE_TRUNC(
            'month',
            created_at
        )::date AS month,

        COUNT(*) AS new_users

    FROM v_analytics_users

    GROUP BY month
),

with_previous AS (
    SELECT
        month,
        new_users,

        LAG(new_users) OVER (
            ORDER BY month
        ) AS previous_month_users

    FROM monthly_users
)

SELECT
    month,
    new_users,
    previous_month_users,

    ROUND(
        100.0
        * (
            new_users
            - previous_month_users
        )
        / NULLIF(
            previous_month_users,
            0
        ),
        2
    ) AS mom_growth_percent

FROM with_previous

ORDER BY month;


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 4
-- MONTHLY POST ACTIVITY + MOM GROWTH
-- =========================================================

WITH monthly_posts AS (
    SELECT
        DATE_TRUNC(
            'month',
            created_at
        )::date AS month,

        COUNT(*) AS total_posts

    FROM v_analytics_posts

    GROUP BY month
),

with_previous AS (
    SELECT
        month,
        total_posts,

        LAG(total_posts) OVER (
            ORDER BY month
        ) AS previous_month_posts

    FROM monthly_posts
)

SELECT
    month,
    total_posts,
    previous_month_posts,

    ROUND(
        100.0
        * (
            total_posts
            - previous_month_posts
        )
        / NULLIF(
            previous_month_posts,
            0
        ),
        2
    ) AS mom_growth_percent

FROM with_previous

ORDER BY month;


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 5
-- MONTHLY POST STATUS MIX
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    DATE_TRUNC(
        'month',
        created_at
    )::date AS month,

    COUNT(*) AS total_posts,

    COUNT(*) FILTER (
        WHERE status = 'published'
    ) AS published_posts,

    COUNT(*) FILTER (
        WHERE status = 'draft'
    ) AS draft_posts,

    COUNT(*) FILTER (
        WHERE status = 'archived'
    ) AS archived_posts,

    ROUND(
        100.0
        * COUNT(*) FILTER (
            WHERE status = 'published'
        )
        / COUNT(*),
        2
    ) AS published_rate_percent

FROM v_analytics_posts

GROUP BY month

ORDER BY month;


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 6
-- CONTENT CREATOR RANKING
-- =========================================================

WITH creator_stats AS (
    SELECT
        u.id,
        u.name,
        COUNT(p.id) AS total_posts

    FROM v_analytics_users u

    LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
        ON p.user_id = u.id

    GROUP BY
        u.id,
        u.name
)

SELECT
    id,
    name,
    total_posts,

    RANK() OVER (
        ORDER BY total_posts DESC
    ) AS creator_rank

FROM creator_stats

ORDER BY
    creator_rank,
    id

LIMIT 20;


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 7
-- TOP 10% CREATOR CONTENT CONTRIBUTION
-- =========================================================

WITH creator_stats AS (
    SELECT
        u.id,
        COUNT(p.id) AS total_posts

    FROM v_analytics_users u

    LEFT JOIN v_analytics_posts p
        ON p.user_id = u.id

    GROUP BY u.id
),

ranked_creators AS (
    SELECT
        id,
        total_posts,

        NTILE(10) OVER (
            ORDER BY total_posts DESC
        ) AS creator_decile

    FROM creator_stats
)

SELECT
    SUM(total_posts) FILTER (
        WHERE creator_decile = 1
    ) AS top_10_percent_posts,

    SUM(total_posts)
        AS all_posts,

    ROUND(
        100.0
        * SUM(total_posts) FILTER (
            WHERE creator_decile = 1
        )
        / NULLIF(
            SUM(total_posts),
            0
        ),
        2
    ) AS top_10_percent_content_share

FROM ranked_creators;


-- =========================================================
-- KPI 8
-- MONTHLY ACTIVE CREATORS
-- Active creator = user created >= 1 post in month
-- =========================================================

SELECT
    DATE_TRUNC(
        'month',
        created_at
    )::date AS month,

    COUNT(
        DISTINCT user_id
    ) AS active_creators,

    COUNT(*) AS total_posts,

    ROUND(
        COUNT(*)::numeric
        / NULLIF(
            COUNT(DISTINCT user_id),
            0
        ),
        2
    ) AS posts_per_active_creator

FROM v_analytics_posts

GROUP BY month

ORDER BY month;
```
